That re-read uses the RETRIED `trader._position_count` — acceptable at 0.01 contracts in a human-supervised tool, and never to be copied into `trader._confirm_v2_no_mapping`, whose read must stay single-shot. **A 2xx order body that is not a JSON object is handled the same way, one step earlier again (DR-58):** `_fill_counts` and `_report_fee` both `.get()` the body, so `"accepted"`, `[]`, `123`, `true` or `null` raised an uncaught `AttributeError` straight after a real ask, skipping the position read, every flatten warning, and the reduce-only close. `v2_probe._non_object_body_fail` now guards both submission-response READERS — the probe submits three orders but only the NO buy and the unfillable ask read their body, since the NO close's response is only `_emit`'d and its verdict comes from `trader._position_count` — restoring the position read (with its own re-read, on a flat OR unreadable first result) and the warning but NOT the close — see the DR-05 paragraph for why the probe is carved out of the rule that keeps the `trader.py` order readers loud, and why the close stays unsubmitted. **A body that IS a JSON object but whose `fill_count`/`remaining_count` cannot be read is the same epistemic state one step later, and in `_step_no_mapping`'s NO buy it now takes the same remedy (DR-60).** That branch used to decide from a SINGLE un-refreshed position read — DR-21's re-read is gated on `filled`, which is `None` and therefore falsy exactly there — and printed a warning only when that read was truthy, so a lagging ledger and a FAILED lookup both came out as silence while a real 0.01 NO position could be open. The re-read-and-report tail is extracted as `v2_probe._recheck_and_report_position(client, ticker, observed)`, ONE definition — since DR-20 below, called from three branches, and the helper's own docstring is the authority on which: it re-reads once after `trader._V2_MAPPING_RECHECK_DELAY_SECONDS` when the caller's first read is `None` or `0` (a read that already moved is evidence and is not re-polled), then prints lookup-failed / position-open / genuinely-flat as three distinct outcomes. The verdict and the exit code are unchanged (`_FAIL`, 1), and the `filled is True` / `filled is False` branches — including DR-21's own re-read and the kill NEUTRAL — are untouched. **Scope, recorded rather than implied: DR-58's guard covers BOTH submission-response readers, DR-60's covers only the NO buy.** `_step_unfillable_ask` has a structurally identical unreadable-fill-counts branch that returns `_FAIL` above its only post-submission position read, so a 2xx object body with no readable fill counts there still prints one FAIL line and never looks at the account. That is a real residual, not a stated trade-off — the step's own DR-58 comment already argues that "an unreadable 2xx body is not proof that it was killed", the same reasoning that would apply here — and it was left alone only because that step was explicitly out of DR-60's scope. Three further branches sit in the same state and also do not call the helper: `_step_unfillable_ask`'s `not killed` branch — the direct sibling of DR-20's new one below, which names the counts and FAILs but reports the account from a single un-refreshed read and never says FLATTEN — and both steps' post-submission EXCEPTION handlers, where `_step_no_mapping`'s bare `if after_err:` collapses a failed lookup into "flat". Do not read the helper's "single definition" wording as coverage of any of these four. **A READABLE response can be impossible too, and `_step_no_mapping` used to call it a clean kill (DR-20).** `filled = remaining == 0 and fill == PROBE_COUNT` collapsed every non-full outcome into one boolean, so a PARTIAL fill (0.005 filled, 0.005 remaining against a count of 0.01) took the `not filled` branch — where DR-21's re-read does not run, because that re-read is gated on `filled` — and, with a ledger that had not caught up, printed "the fill-or-kill was killed unfilled and the account is still flat", exited NEUTRAL (code 2) and gave no flatten warning while a real 0.005 position was open on the production account; `_report_fee`'s absent-`average_fee_paid` line added a second, independent "(nothing filled)" claim just above it. The step now carries a separate `conforming` flag — `filled or (fill == 0 and remaining == PROBE_COUNT)` — and anything else (partial, over-fill, stale remainder) is the fill-or-kill INVARIANT VIOLATION this probe exists to catch: it FAILs, names the actual `fill_count`/`remaining_count`, points at `ORDER_API_VERSION = "legacy"`, and reports the account through the same shared `_recheck_and_report_position` tail. This is the ONE `filled is False` change DR-60 left untouched; the TRUE-kill NEUTRAL (verdict pinned by `tests/test_v2_probe.py::TestNoMappingVerdict::test_no_fill_is_neutral_and_submits_no_unwind`, wording by `::TestNonConformingFillOrKill::test_a_true_kill_with_a_flat_account_is_still_neutral`), the `filled is True` branches and DR-21's own re-read are all unchanged. `_report_fee` no longer asserts "nothing filled" — it reads no fill counts and so cannot say. The sibling `_step_unfillable_ask` already classified this way (`killed = fill == 0 and remaining == PROBE_COUNT`, FAILing a partial and printing the counts), so the two steps now apply the SAME kill test — their remedies still differ, since that step's ask is meant to be unfillable, so it folds a complete fill into the same FAIL and reports the account from one un-refreshed read. **The probe deliberately holds a STRICTER kill contract than `trader._v2_fill_status`, and the FAIL text says so rather than claim the live path would have caught the same body.** That reader consults ONLY `fill_count` (it never reads `remaining_count` anywhere in `trader.py`), so it genuinely raises on a partial or an over-fill — `fill_count` neither the requested count nor zero — but a STALE REMAINDER (`fill_count == count` with a non-zero `remaining_count`) books as `"executed"` there and a kill whose `remaining_count` is not the full count books as `"canceled"`. The probe reads both counts because an unexpected `remaining_count` is itself a finding on the one gate that decides whether the bot stays on V2, and because matching `_step_unfillable_ask`'s long-standing test is what makes the two steps agree. The practical consequence to expect on a first live run: a genuine kill reporting `fill_count=0` with a zeroed rather than full `remaining_count` is now a FAIL naming both counts, where it used to be a NEUTRAL with a retry hint — and it would have FAILed the kill-semantics step regardless, since `_step_unfillable_ask` has required the full remainder since it was written. **And `--dest-shard` may not name the source shard (DR-22).** It is declared `type=int` with no `choices`, and `_step_transfer`'s two guards — is the shard advertised, are its transfers active — are both satisfied by shard 0 BY CONSTRUCTION, since it IS `_TRANSFER_SOURCE_SHARD`; so `--dest-shard 0` POSTed a real, non-idempotent, never-retried transfer with `source_exchange_shard == destination_exchange_shard`. **Derived from source, not observed** (the probe was never run this way): the POST is what the bug is, and IF the exchange accepts it, a net-zero move cannot raise that shard's balance by the probe cent `_await_transfer_settlement`'s target demands, so the poll burns the full `TRANSFER_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and reports a FALSE `MONEY MAY BE IN FLIGHT`; if the exchange 400s it instead, the step FAILs on the POST with a different message. The refusal is a NEUTRAL at the TOP of `_step_transfer`, before any transfer I/O (`main()` still builds the client and reads the balance before dispatching to any step) — not in argparse — because `_step_transfer` is what spends the money and is reachable from `main()`'s step dispatch and from any future caller, not only from the CLI. Negative and unknown destinations need no separate check: they are not advertised, so the existing advertised-shard guard already short-circuits them to NEUTRAL.
- **Omitted optional fields rely on server defaults:** `self_trade_prevention_type`, `subaccount`, and `cancel_order_on_pause` are deliberately not sent.
- Other fixed choices: prices are 4-decimal dollar strings (`_format_price`, exactly representing every grid point of every regime); counts are fixed-point strings (`_format_count`); each order body's `exchange_index` is that leg's OWN market's `exchange_index` (the NO leg and its rollback from the NO leg's market — `market_a` for same-title, `market_b` for time-series — and the YES leg from the YES leg's market; a pair's legs can sit on different shards) and never `-1` auto-route, so a wrong shard model is rejected loudly by the exchange instead of silently routed, and an explicit-shard write bills only that shard's rate-limit bucket where auto-route bills every nonzero shard's. The legacy endpoint has no shard-routing parameter at all, so while `ORDER_API_VERSION` is not `"v2"`, `trader._legacy_routable()` refuses any spec with a leg off `DEFAULT_EXCHANGE_INDEX` at the top of `_execute_one()` — `status="failed"`, since nothing was submitted and there is nothing to unwind; `post_only` is always `False` (we are deliberate takers) and `reduce_only` is `True` only on the rollback.

---

## Documentation Standards

**Every file must have a header comment block** at the top (after the shebang/encoding if any) following the pattern already established in every module:
```python
"""
File: filename.py
Author: <name>
Last edited by: <name>

Purpose:
    One paragraph describing what this module does and why it exists.

Dependencies:
    What it imports from the project and what imports from it.

Notes:
    Any non-obvious constraints, SDK quirks, or gotchas specific to this file.
"""
```

**Every function must have a Google-style docstring** matching the existing style:
```python
def my_function(arg: type) -> ReturnType:
    """
    One-sentence summary of what the function does.

    Longer explanation if the logic is non-trivial. Include the invariants
    this function assumes and what it guarantees.

    Args:
        arg (type): Description. Include valid range if numeric.

    Returns:
        type: Description of what's returned and when.

    Raises:
        ExceptionType: When and why this is raised.
    """
```
Functions that return `None` only on failure should document that: `Returns None if <condition>.`

**Every cross-module function call must have an inline comment** explaining the *why*, not just the *what*. The existing codebase already follows this for critical calls (see `main.py` and `strategy.py`):
```python
# Validate depth & replace best-ask with depth-weighted fill price
pairs = scanner.enrich_with_orderbook_prices(client, pairs)
```
Single-line calls to stdlib or well-known helpers (e.g. `logging.info(...)`, `sorted(...)`) don't need comments unless the arguments are surprising.

**Update the README and all relevant comments whenever a change affects behavior.** Specifically:
- If you add, remove, or rename a module: update the Module Dependency Graph in `README.md` and the Module Map in `CLAUDE.md`.
- If you change a constant in `config.py`: update the comment explaining the constant's purpose and rationale.
- If you add a new CLI argument to `main.py` or `backtest.py`: update the Run Commands section in `README.md` and in `CLAUDE.md`.
- If you change the data flow of the live or backtest pipeline: update the corresponding ASCII diagram in `README.md`.
- If you change function signatures or behavior: update the docstring in the same commit, not as a follow-up.

---

## What NOT To Do

- **Do not bypass the rollback logic in `trader._execute_one()`**. If the YES leg is *confirmed* unfilled after the NO leg fills — a clean FoK rejection, or an error whose position delta is 0 on the first read AND on the lag re-read (DR-63) — an unwind must be attempted immediately: an orphaned NO-leg position (`market_a` for same-title, `market_b` for time-series) is an unhedged one-sided position. But an unwind requires *confirmation*, not a guess. When a leg errors and the delta is unattributable (the lookup failed, or the position moved by an amount the order can't explain), the correct behavior is `status="manual_review"` with no order submitted — on the YES leg because an unwind could reverse a real fill, and on the NO leg because a `reduce_only` sell could liquidate an unrelated holding. Do not "restore" the NO leg's old unconditional unwind-on-unknown.
- **Do not add retry logic to order submission** — this covers `trader._submit_order` (legacy, via `fetch_json_page`), `trader._submit_order_v2` (V2, via `_http.signed_request_json`) and `trader._execute_transfer` (the non-idempotent collateral POST) equally. One retry after a fill-or-kill rejection could submit the NO leg twice at different prices, creating an unhedged position, and a retried transfer moves the money twice. **The read-only position lookups in `trader._position_count` ARE retried and must stay that way** — a GET cannot duplicate a trade, but an unretried transient 429 there turns a resolvable ambiguity into a rollback or `manual_review`. Assert the rule per-function on the submission/transfer sources, never as a module-wide "trader.py must not import `api_call_with_retry`" ban.
- **Do not unwind the NO leg with an unpriced order on either path.** The legacy unwind is a floored FoK LIMIT sell and the V2 unwind is a floored (bid-capped) FoK limit bid; both express the same `ROLLBACK_MAX_LOSS_CENTS_PER_CONTRACT` bound. A market-type (or top-of-grid-flat) unwind has no loss floor at all. (This rule is about the TRADE unwind in `trader.py` and is absolute there. `v2_probe._no_close_body`'s closing bid is deliberately top-of-grid-flat and is not a trade unwind — it is a 0.01-contract `reduce_only` probe order whose whole point is to fill; see the V2-semantics bullet above before "restoring" the builder's price to it.)
- **Do not hardcode `spec.pair.market_a` as the NO leg, or `market_a`'s side as NO, anywhere.** `config.SAME_TITLE_LEG_SIDES`/`TIME_SERIES_LEG_SIDES` via `scanner.leg_sides()`, `scanner.leg_prices()` and `trader._ordered_legs()` are the only source of truth for which side a leg buys, which price it pays, and which leg is submitted first and unwound; for a time-series pair the NO leg is `market_b`.
- **Do not describe, price or pay a fourth time-series settlement cell.** A=YES/B=NO is impossible for a cumulative-deadline pair; the backtester excludes and counts it as a premise violation. No table, docstring, log string or README/CLAUDE.md sentence may assign that cell a payout of any kind; the only double-payout cell anywhere is the SAME-TITLE table's NY cell in `_settlement_receipt`.
- **Do not apply the one-series rule to only one finder, and do not replace it with a close-time gate.** `scanner._same_series` must gate BOTH `find_same_title_pairs` and (conjoined with `_identical_wording`) `find_time_series_pairs`, and `backtester._extract_pairs` must mirror it on both of its branches — the same two tickers qualify for both pair types, so a one-sided gate relabels the trade instead of stopping it (pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_apply_the_one_series_rule`). A close-gap threshold is not a SUBSTITUTE for it: on the corpus that rejection was measured on, identical-wording non-combo pairs (same-series and cross-series pooled) co-resolved 96.3–98.2% of the time in every close-gap band, while combo pairs closing 96 seconds apart still disagreed 13.8% of the time — so for the ONE-SERIES case, fixture identity separates them and proximity does not. That verdict is about the same-series case only: on DR-74's 365-day corpus, CROSS-series identical-wording pairs settled the same way 99.2% of the time at a 0 s close gap against 60.7% beyond 1 minute, so there proximity DOES separate them (see the DR-02 gotcha's annotation and the close-gate gotcha). DR-74's close gate is not a replacement either — it is an ADDITIONAL condition on the cross-series same-title pairs the series rule already admits (see the next bullet), and the one-series rule stays in front of it on both paths.
- **Do not apply the same-title close gate on only one PATH, and do not add it to `find_time_series_pairs` (DR-74).** `scanner.closes_apart` must gate BOTH `scanner.find_same_title_pairs` (through `_closes_apart`) and `backtester._extract_pairs`' same-title branch (through `_comparable_closes_dicts`) — one definition, pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_paths_apply_the_same_title_close_gap` and `::test_ast_the_close_gap_has_one_definition` — or the backtest replays the men's/women's trades the live path refuses, exactly as it reproduced DR-01 before the group key was shared. It belongs on the same-title finder ONLY: identical wording can never form a time-series pair (DR-67), so a same-title-only gate cannot relabel the trade, and a close gate on the time-series finder would refuse every genuine cross-event deadline pair, whose closes differ by design (the same AST pin asserts its absence there). Keep it failing CLOSED on a close it cannot compare, keep it AFTER the series test so the older counts do not move, and keep the backtest's unreadable-close count on its own line so the close-gap line stays the live one's verbatim twin.
- **Do not apply the cumulative-deadline rule to only one path, and do not let a snapshot pair through.** `scanner.cumulative_deadline_pair` (over `scanner.deadline_phrasing`) must gate BOTH `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` and `backtester._extract_pairs`' time-series branch — one shared helper, only the field extraction differing (pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_apply_the_cumulative_deadline_rule` and `::test_ast_the_deadline_classifier_has_one_definition`). Gating only the live path would leave the backtester replaying the defect instead of detecting it, exactly as it reproduced DR-01 before the grouping key was shared. The rule belongs on the time-series branch ONLY — a same-title pair has no deadline concept — and it must keep failing CLOSED: wording that names no deadline is refused, because the YES-on-earlier/NO-on-later trade has no premise without the nesting a cumulative deadline provides. Do not move it into `_can_ever_enter` for speed: that is a MARKET-eligibility predicate named by `config.SETTLED_PREFILTER_CACHE_TAG`, and putting it there silently corrupts every assembled cache. Do not merge the phrasing tables into `_DATE_PATTERNS` — one erases dates, the other reads the preposition around them.
- **Do not order or tier a same-event ladder on `close_time`, and do not apply the ladder rule to only one path (DR-73).** A ladder's legs are ordered by `scanner.stated_deadline` and gapped by `scanner.same_event_ladder`, and that stated gap is what chooses the tier, is checked against `MAX_DEADLINE_GAP_DAYS`, travels on `CandidatePair.stated_gap_days` and is read downstream only through `scanner.pair_gap_days` — because a settled or single-instant event closes every rung together (681 of 1,821 archive same-event pairs have a close gap of ZERO days and 13 are ordered the wrong way round by realized close), so `close_time` silently re-tiers such a pair from the 0.70 ceiling to 0.85 or inverts its legs. The rule must gate BOTH `scanner.find_time_series_pairs` and `backtester._extract_pairs`/`_find_entry`, on the SAME `TIME_SERIES_SAME_EVENT_LADDERS` resolved at call time — each module's own by-value binding of the config constant, so a test or harness patches `scanner.` / `backtester.` / `backtest.` and never `config.`, which none of them reads — never on `event_ticker` equality alone, which would hand ladder semantics to any caller regardless of the switch — and it is pinned by `tests/test_strategy.py::TestTimeSeriesKellyParity::test_ast_both_finders_apply_the_same_event_ladder_rule` and `::test_ast_pair_ceiling_reads_the_pair_gap`. A one-sided rule would let a ladder-enabled live run be measured by a backtest that refuses every same-event pair, the same relabelling trap the one-series rule records. It must keep failing CLOSED at every step: an empty shared event ticker, identical wording, a non-cumulative verdict, a deadline stating no placeable calendar day, fields naming irreconcilable days, both rungs naming ONE day, or a stated gap over the cap all refuse the pair. Branch on `is None` / `is SAME_DAY`, never truthiness — `SAME_DAY` is a non-empty string.
- **Do not import `trader.py` from `scanner.py` or `strategy.py`** — circular dependency.
- **Do not write output files outside `PROJECT_ROOT`**. All logs, Excel files, and HTML dashboards write relative to `PROJECT_ROOT`.
- **Do not modify `_DATE_PATTERNS` without running `test_normalize_title`** — a regression silently causes missed pairs or false positives with no error.
- **Do not import `v2_probe` from any pipeline module** — it is a human-run tool that submits real-money orders; a single import would put probe submissions one code path away from the weekly scheduler (a test asserts this).
- **Do not add tests that call the real Kalshi API**. Use `unittest.mock.MagicMock` for `KalshiClient`. Tests should be runnable offline.
- **Do not change the fee formula without updating both** `fee_per_pair_approx()` and `fee_leg_exact()` in `config.py`. The backtester also uses both.
- **Do not take the fee out of Kelly's denominator, and do not merge Kelly's `b` with the reported `profit_ratio` (DR-62).** `b` divides by `price_a + price_b + fee_per_pair_approx(...)` — the dollars a losing pair actually forfeits — in all three sizers (`strategy._evaluate_size`'s `kelly_b`, `backtester._simulate_at_discount`'s `kelly_b_entry`, `dashboard._kelly_fraction`'s `b`); dropping the fee there overstates modelled EV by exactly `q·fee`, so `f* > 0` stops meaning positive expected value even under the continuous fee approximation the gate prices with. (With the fee in place it means positive EV under that approximation and nothing more — at single-digit `n` the ceiling-rounded exact fee can still leave an accepted spec marginally EV-negative on its own fields; never restate the guarantee without that qualifier.) The fee-less ratio beside it is the REPORTED return on contract cost and feeds `select_portfolio`'s ranking key — two names, two quantities, on purpose.
